But the processes are running. This is definitely Python buffering. The evaluate.py script is using logging, and the output is redirected to a file. In Python, when you redirect stdout, it becomes fully buffered by default. The `nohup` command also redirects stderr. Since logging.basicConfig uses stderr by default but here it might be configured to use stdout. Let me look at the evaluate.py code again - it uses logging which goes to stderr, but stderr is redirected to the same file with 2>&1. Python's default behavior buffers both stdout and stderr when not connected to a terminal.\n\nThe solution would have been to use `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`, but since I already started the processes, I can't change that now. \n\nI'll just have to wait for the processes to complete and flush their output
